\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}s}(y))}

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{s}(y)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{7s}(y)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

This ends the study of the two cases. We now apply Theorem
2.5
with
\lambda:=56
,
Q_{0}:=Q_{r}(x),\ q:=\infty,\ \mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]

and
f:=\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[C_{\theta}F^{\varepsilon}]
.
Moreover,

F_{Q}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]-\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla w_{s}],\quad&s\geq 4t,&\\
&0,\quad&0<s<4t,&\end{aligned}\right.

and

R_{Q}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla w_{s}],\quad&s\geq 4t,&\\
&\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}],\quad&0<s<4t.&\end{aligned}\right.

For any given
p>2
, we may choose
\theta
sufficiently small with
C\theta^{\sigma}<\kappa_{0}
so that the requirement of Theorem
2.5
is satisfied. Consequently, we arrive at

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(x)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{56r}(x)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{56r}(x)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[C_{\theta}F^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}

(2.29)

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\theta(p))
and
\varepsilon/(4\theta)\leq t\leq r
and
Q_{57r}(x)\subset Q_{1}(0)
.
Estimate (
2.10
) now follows by a covering argument (see the proof of Lemma
2.2
) and Lemma
2.1
(in order to adjust the size of balls and relax the condition
t\geq(4\theta)
to
t\geq\varepsilon
). To remove the smallness condition
\varepsilon\in(0,\theta(p))
, we observe that the case
\theta(p)\leq\varepsilon\leq t\leq r\leq\frac{1}{2}
is trivial as the constant
C
is allowed to depend on
p
.
∎

2.2.
Bootstrap argument

In this subsection, we apply the large-scale Calderón-Zygmund estimate proved previously to study the regularity of the stationary Navier-Stokes equations (
NSε
). Note that in Theorem
2.4
,
F^{\varepsilon}
is a general function. We will take advantage of the nonlinearity
F^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}
. As usual, the proof relies on a bootstrap argument.

Throughout this subsection, we set
F^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}
. To begin with, note that the Sobolev embedding theorem implies
F^{\varepsilon}\in L^{3}
, which yields
\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]\in L^{3}
. Hence, (
2.10
) holds with
p=3
. To further improve the large-scale regularity, we need to lift the regularity of
F^{\varepsilon}
from that of
\nabla u^{\varepsilon}
.

For any
0\leq a<b\leq\infty
, define a new maximal function

\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{M}_{(a,b)}^{1}[g](x)=\sup_{a<t<b}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|g|.

Note that
\mathcal{M}_{(0,\infty)}^{1}
is the usual Hardy-Littlewood maximal function. Clearly, by the
L^{p}
boundedness of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function,
\mathcal{M}_{(a,b)}^{1}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}
space for
p\in(1,\infty)
.

Fix
t>0
. Define

K_{q}(r)=K_{q,t}(r)=\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(0)}\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

The following estimate is a sort of the large-scale Sobolev embedding theorem.

Theorem 2.7
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
\varepsilon\leq t\leq r\leq\frac{1}{7}
and
F^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}
. Then for any
p>3
and any
q
satisfying

\frac{1}{q}<\frac{1}{2p}+\frac{1}{3},

(2.30)

we have

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(0)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\leq Cr^{2}\big(K_{q}(5r)\big)^{2},

(2.31)

where the constant
C
depends only
L
,
p
and
q
.

Proof.

Let
p>3
and
q
satisfy (
2.30
). Without loss of generality, we assume in addition
\frac{1}{2p}<\frac{1}{q}
.
Let
x\in B_{r,+}^{\varepsilon}(0)
.
We first estimate

\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}](x)=\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|F^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Let
x=(x^{\prime},x_{3})
. We consider the cases
x_{3}\geq t
and
x_{3}<t
separately. Assume first
x_{3}\geq t
and let
N
be the natural number so that
2^{N-1}t<x_{3}\leq 2^{N}t
.
Note that
u^{\varepsilon}
vanishes in a large portion of
Q_{2^{N+1}t}(x)
. By the triangle inequality and the Poincaré inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2t}(x)}u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/4}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j}t}(x)}u^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}u^{\varepsilon}\bigg|+\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{N+1}t}(x)}u^{\varepsilon}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{N}2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Now, let
\alpha\in(0,\min\{\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{q}\})

and write

\displaystyle 2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(2.32)

\displaystyle\leq C2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{\alpha}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q-\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C(2^{j+1}t)^{1-3\alpha}\bigg(\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{\alpha}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q-\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C(2^{j+1}t)^{1-3\alpha}r^{3\alpha}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{5r}}(0)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{\alpha}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q-\alpha}.

Using the definition of
K_{q}
and
\mathcal{M}^{1}_{(2t,{5r})}
, we obtain

\displaystyle 2^{j+1}t\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2^{j+1}t}(x)}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C(2^{j+1}t)^{1-3\alpha}r^{3\alpha}(K_{q}({5r}))^{\alpha q}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{(2t,{5r})}^{1}\big[\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\big](x)\Big)^{1/q-\alpha}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{N}(2^{j+1}t)^{1-3\alpha}r^{3\alpha}(K_{q}({5r}))^{\alpha q}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{(2t,{5r})}^{1}\big[\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\big](x)\Big)^{1/q-\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq Cr(K_{q}({5r}))^{\alpha q}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{(2t,{5r})}^{1}\big[\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}\big](x)\Big)^{1/q-\alpha},

which yields

\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}](x)\leq Cr^{2}(K_{q}({5r}))^{2\alpha q}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{(2t,{5r})}^{1}[\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}](x)\Big)^{2/q-2\alpha}.

(2.33)

On the other hand, if
x_{3}<t
, then
B_{2t}(x)
has a relatively large portion not contained in
\Omega^{\varepsilon}
. Thus, the Sobolev-Poincaré inequality implies

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/4}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2t}(x)}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{4}\bigg)^{1/4}\leq Ct\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2t}(x)}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Using the same argument as (
2.32
), we see that
\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}](x)
has the same bound as (
2.33
) for
x_{3}<t
.

Since by assumption,
\frac{1}{2p}<\frac{1}{q}<\frac{1}{2p}+\frac{1}{3}
and
0<\alpha<\min\{\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{q}\}
is arbitrary, we may choose
\alpha
so that
\frac{1}{q}>\frac{1}{2p}+\alpha
.
This implies
p(\frac{2}{q}-2\alpha)>1
. Thus, using the
L^{p(\frac{2}{q}-2\alpha)}
boundedness of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}(0)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}](x)|^{p}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2p}(K_{q}({5r}))^{2\alpha pq}\int_{Q_{r}(0)}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{(2t,{5r})}^{1}[\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{q}](x)\Big)^{p(2/q-2\alpha)}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2p}(K_{q}({5r}))^{2\alpha pq}\int_{Q_{{5r}}(0)}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}](x)\Big)^{2p(1-\alpha q)}\,{\rm d}x.

Consequently,

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(0)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\leq Cr^{2}(K_{q}({5r}))^{{2\alpha q}}(K_{2p(1-\alpha q)}({5r}))^{2(1-\alpha q)}.

Now, observe that we may choose
\alpha<\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2p}
but sufficiently close to
\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2p}
. Then
q<2p(1-\alpha q)\to q
as
\alpha
approaches
\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2p}
. This implies

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}(0)}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}\leq Cr^{2}\big(K_{2p(1-2\alpha)}({5r})\big)^{2}\leq Cr^{2}\big(K_{\hat{q}}({5r})\big)^{2}.

(2.34)

for any
\hat{q}>q
,
where we also used the fact that
K_{m}(r)\leq K_{n}(r)
for any
1\leq m\leq n
. Finally, to recover the case with the exact exponent
q
, we may start with a
\tilde{q}<q
still satisfying
\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}<\frac{1}{2p}+\frac{1}{3}
.
Then (
2.34
) holds for any
\hat{q}>\tilde{q}
, which includes the case
\hat{q}=q
. This proves the desired estimate.
∎

Now, a bootstrap argument between (
2.10
) and (
2.31
) shows that both
\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]
and
\mathcal{M}_{t}^{2}[F^{\varepsilon}]
are in
L^{p}
for any
p\geq 3
.
In the following, we use this to prove a large-scale Hölder’s estimate for
F^{\varepsilon}
, which plays an important role in the Lipschitz estimate in the next section.

Theorem 2.8
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
\varepsilon\leq t\leq r\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Let
M\geq 0
be such that

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{1,+}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq M.

For every
l>3
and
\delta>0
satisfying
l\delta<6
, we have

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}\leq Cr^{2-6/l}\big(M+M^{2(4-6/l+\delta)}\big),

(2.35)

where the constant
C
depends only on

L
,
l
and
\delta
.

Proof.

Note that, by a similar argument as at the end of the proof of Theorem
2.4
, we only have to prove (
2.35
) when
\varepsilon/N_{0}\leq t\leq r\leq 1/N_{1}
for some
N_{0},N_{1}\geq 2
.
Let
l>3
and
\delta>0
with
l\delta<6
be given and fixed for the proof.
First of all, by the Sobolev embedding theorem,
\|F^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq CM^{2}
. This implies

\|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1/2})}\leq CM^{2}
. By Theorem
2.4
,

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/8}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}\leq C(M+M^{2}).

Then, applying Theorem
2.7
, we obtain that for any
3\leq p<\infty
,

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/40}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/{p}}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/8}}}\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]^{3}\bigg)^{2/3}\leq C(M+M^{4}).

Now, using Theorem
2.4
again combined with a covering argument, we derive from the last inequality that

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/80}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{1/{p}}\leq C(M+M^{4}).

Now, let
p>l
. By the interpolation, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/80}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{l}\bigg)^{1/l}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/80}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{\theta/3}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/80}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{p}\bigg)^{(1-\theta)/p}

(2.36)

\displaystyle\leq C(M+M^{2})^{\theta}(M+M^{4})^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C(M+M^{4-2\theta}),

where

\frac{1}{l}=\frac{\theta}{3}+\frac{1-\theta}{p}.

For the given
\delta\in(0,1)
, we want
4-2\theta=4-6/l+\delta
. This implies
\theta=3/l-\delta/2
and thus we may choose

p=\frac{6(1-\frac{3}{l}+\frac{\delta}{2})}{\delta}.

One can easily verify that
\theta\in(0,1)
by the assumption on
l
and
\delta
.
Consequently, we derive from (
2.36
) that

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{1/80}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{l}\bigg)^{1/l}\leq C\big(M+M^{4-6/{l}+\delta}\big).

Finally, we apply Theorem
2.7
to obtain for
r\leq 1/400
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{{5r}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{l}\bigg)^{2/l}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2-6/l}\bigg(\int_{Q_{{5r}}}|\mathcal{M}_{t}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{l}\bigg)^{2/l}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2-6/l}\big(M+M^{2(4-6/l+\delta)}\big).

This ends the proof.
∎

Note that if
\nabla u^{\varepsilon}
itself is in
L^{p}
for
p>3
, then Morrey’s inequality implies that
u^{\varepsilon}
is
C^{0,1-3/p}
, which implies, since
u^{\varepsilon}
vanishes on the boundary,

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r}}|F^{\varepsilon}|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}\leq Cr^{2-6/p},

where
C
depends on
M
,
L
and
p
. Hence, (
2.35
) is consistent with the usual Morrey estimate.

3.
Large-scale Lipschitz estimate

In this section, we will establish the large-scale Lipschitz estimate of
u^{\varepsilon}
and the oscillation estimate of
p^{\varepsilon}
. We remark, for later use in Subsection
4.2
, that Theorem
A
implies the following Liouville theorem for the Stokes system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta u+\nabla p=0&\text{in }\Omega\\
\nabla\cdot u=0&\text{in }\Omega\\
u=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

(3.1)

where
\Omega
is a John domain in Definition
1.2
. The proof of the following statement is standard.

Corollary 3.1
.

Let
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain according to Definition
1.2
. Let
(u,p)
be a weak solution of (
3.1
).
If

\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{R}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{R}(0)\cap\Omega}|u|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}=0,

then
u\equiv 0
(hence
p
is constant).

3.1.
Sep-up and approximation

First of all, we may write (
NSε
) as a linear Stokes system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta u^{\varepsilon}+\nabla p^{\varepsilon}={\nabla\cdot F^{\varepsilon}}&\mbox{in}\ B^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\\
\nabla\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0&\mbox{in}\ B^{\varepsilon}_{1,+}\\
u^{\varepsilon}=0&\mbox{on}\ \Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{1},\end{array}\right.

(S
ε
)

where
F^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}
. As in the classical regularity theory for Stokes system, we will use the large-scale
C^{0,\alpha}
estimate of
F^{\varepsilon}
in Theorem
2.8
to prove the large-scale Lipschitz estimate. The proof is based on the excess decay method.

Similarly to the large-scale Calderón-Zygmund estimate of Theorem
2.4
, we also need to approximate the Stokes system (
Sε
) at all scales greater than
\varepsilon
.
Fix
r\in[\varepsilon,{\frac{1}{2}}]
and let
(v_{r},q_{r})
be the weak solution of the following Stokes system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta v_{r}+\nabla q_{r}=0&\mbox{in}\ Q^{\varepsilon}_{t_{0}r}\\
\nabla\cdot v_{r}=0&\mbox{in}\ Q^{\varepsilon}_{t_{0}r}\\
v_{r}=u^{\varepsilon}&\mbox{on}\ \partial Q^{\varepsilon}_{t_{0}r},\end{array}\right.

(S
r
)

where we have automatically extended
u^{\varepsilon}
across the bottom boundary by zero-extension and
t_{0}
is a constant in the interval
[1,2]
chosen analogously as in (
2.11
) and (
2.12
). Note that (
Sr
) is a special case of (
2.12
) with
s=r
and
y=0
, which means the estimates (
2.13
)-(
2.15
) hold also for
(v_{r},q_{r})
, in place of
(w_{s},q_{s})
.
The following lemma is an analog of Lemma
2.6
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
.
Let
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon})
and
(v_{r},q_{r})
be weak solutions of (
Sε
) and (
Sr
), respectively. If
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10}]
and
r\in[2\varepsilon,\frac{1}{5}]
, then

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.2)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/12}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{5r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{4r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3},

where
C
depends only on
L
.

Proof.

Let us set
R^{\varepsilon}_{r}=Q^{\varepsilon}_{t_{0}r}\cap\{-\varepsilon<x_{3}\leq 2\varepsilon\}
.
By examining the proof of Lemma
2.6
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{t_{0}r,+}}|\nabla(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r})|^{2}\leq C\int_{R^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/6}\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{2r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+C\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{2r,+}}|F^{\varepsilon}|^{2}.

(3.3)

From Lemma
2.1
(iii) and Theorem
2.4
with
p=3
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}|}\int_{R^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\frac{1}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}|}\int_{R^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{|R^{\varepsilon}_{r}|}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}|}\big)^{1/6}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[\nabla u^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/6}\bigg\{\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{5r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{4r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}\bigg\}.

Inserting this into (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.4)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/12}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{5r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{4r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}.

Next, we estimate the pressure by using the Bogovskii lemma. The issue is that, in general,
B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}
is not a John domain. By Definition
1.2
, for
r\geq 2\varepsilon
, there exists a John domain
\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}
with constant
L
satisfying

B_{r/2,+}^{\varepsilon}\subset\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}\subset B_{r,+}^{\varepsilon}.

Note that
(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})
satisfies

-\Delta(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r})+\nabla(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})=\nabla\cdot F^{\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{in }B_{r,+}^{\varepsilon}.

Thus, we may use the Bogovskii lemma in
\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}
and (
3.4
) to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.5)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}|F^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/12}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{5r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{4r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3},

where we also used the fact
|\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}|\approx|B_{r,+}^{\varepsilon}|
. Using a well-known fact

\int_{E}|f-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{E}f|^{2}=\inf_{a\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{E}|f-a|^{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for any open set }E,

we derive

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r/2,+}^{\varepsilon}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r/2,+}^{\varepsilon}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.6)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r/2,+}^{\varepsilon}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{r}^{\varepsilon}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Combining (
3.4
), (
3.5
) and (
3.6
), we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

Remark 3.3
.

The pressure estimate in John domains in the proof of Lemma
3.2
is a standard technique that we will frequently use throughout this paper. It allows us to transfer the pressure estimate to the estimates of
\nabla u^{\varepsilon}
and
F^{\varepsilon}
.

3.2.
Excess decay

Let
\mathscr{P}_{1}=\{(ax_{3},bx_{3},0)~|~a,b\in\mathbb{R}\}
. Note that
\mathscr{P}_{1}
consists of all the linear solutions (velocity component) of the Stokes equations in the whole space with the no-slip condition on
\{x_{3}=0\}
. These linear solutions are dubbed as
no-slip Stokes polynomials
of degree
1
.

For a pair of function
(w^{\varepsilon},\pi^{\varepsilon})\in H^{1}(B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+})^{3}\times L^{2}(B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+})
with
r\in(0,1]
, we set

\displaystyle H(w^{\varepsilon},\pi^{\varepsilon};\rho)

\displaystyle=\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\rho,+}}|\nabla w^{\varepsilon}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{s\rho,+}}\pi^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{t\rho,+}}\pi^{\varepsilon}\bigg|,\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in(0,r],

and

\displaystyle\Phi(w^{\varepsilon},\pi^{\varepsilon};\rho)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\rho,+}}|\nabla w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.8)

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{s\rho,+}}\pi^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{t\rho,+}}\pi^{\varepsilon}\bigg|,\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in(0,r].

The quantity
H
can be dubbed as a zeroth-order excess quantity. In Section
5
we will consider higher-order excess quantities
H_{{\rm 1st}}
and
H_{{\rm 2nd}}
to address the large-scale
C^{1,\gamma}
and
C^{2,\gamma}
regularity.

Moreover, for a pair of function
(w_{r},\pi_{r})\in H^{1}(Q^{\varepsilon}_{r})^{3}\times L^{2}(Q^{\varepsilon}_{r})
with
r\in(0,1]
, we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(w_{r},\pi_{r};\rho)

\displaystyle=\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\rho}}|\nabla w_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.9)

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{s\rho}}\pi_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{t\rho}}\pi_{r}\bigg|,\hskip 10.00002pt\rho\in(0,r].

The following lemma states the comparability between
H(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)
and
\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
,
r\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}]
and let
(v_{r},q_{r})
satisfy (
Sr
). Then we have the following statements.

(i)

For all
\theta\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle H(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq C\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)+C\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/2}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.10)

(ii)

For all
\theta\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq CH(v_{r},q_{r};2\theta r)+C\theta^{-5/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.11)

Here
C
depends only on
L
.

Proof.

(i) We first deal with
v_{r}
. Since
B_{\theta r,+}^{\varepsilon}\subset Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}
and
|B_{\theta r,+}^{\varepsilon}|\approx|Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}|
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r,+}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

On the other hand, the triangle inequality implies

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta sr,+}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta tr,+}}q_{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta sr}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta tr}}q_{r}\bigg|

(3.12)

\displaystyle+2\sup_{\rho\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}}q_{r}\bigg|.

Combining the above two inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle H(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)\leq C\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)+2\sup_{\rho\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}}q_{r}\bigg|.

(3.13)

Since
|Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}|
is less than
C\varepsilon(\theta\rho r)^{2}
, a direct computation yields

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}}q_{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{1}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}|}-\frac{1}{|Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}|}\Big)\bigg|\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}}(q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}q_{r})\bigg|

(3.14)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}|}\bigg|\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}}(q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}q_{r})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{|Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}||Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}|^{1/2}}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}||Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{|Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}|^{1/2}|Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}|^{1/2}}{|B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r,+}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}q_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\theta^{-1}\rho^{-5/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)+\theta^{-1/2}\rho^{-2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/2}\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2},

where we have applied the Hölder inequality in the second inequality and the Bogovskii lemma in
Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}
in the third inequality. Noting
\rho\geq\frac{1}{16}
and using (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), we obtain the first inequality (
3.10
).

(ii)
Let
P_{*}\in\mathscr{P}_{1}
be such that

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r,+}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P_{*}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}=\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r,+}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Since
v_{r}(x)-P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})
is a weak solution to (
Sr
) with the same pressure
q_{r}
and
P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})=P_{*}(x)+\varepsilon(\nabla P_{*}){\bf e}_{3}
, by the Bogovskii lemma in
Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}
, we see that

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta sr,+}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta tr,+}}q_{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\rho\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta\rho r}}|q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}q_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.15)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P_{*}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Moreover, by the Caccioppoli inequality (see Lemma
A.3
) in rectangular region
Q_{2\theta r}^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P_{*}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(3.16)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\theta r}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}}|v_{r}-P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\theta r}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|v_{r}-P_{*}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)|\nabla P_{*}|

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(\theta r)^{5/2}}\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}.

Now (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) combined with the Poincaré inequality imply

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)

(3.17)

\displaystyle\leq CH(v_{r},q_{r};2\theta r)+C\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)|\nabla P_{*}|

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(\theta r)^{5/2}}\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}.

By the definition of
P_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla P_{*}|\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.18)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(\theta r)^{5/2}}\bigg\{\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|P_{*}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}

(3.19)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(\theta r)^{5/2}}\bigg\{\varepsilon\bigg(\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}\setminus B^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r,+}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\varepsilon(\varepsilon\theta^{2}r^{2})^{1/2}|\nabla P_{*}|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\theta^{-5/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)+\theta^{-3/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{3/2}\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2\theta r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Hence, we obtain (
3.11
) from (
3.17
) combined with (
3.18
) and (
3.19
). This completes the proof of Lemma
3.4
.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Fix
\alpha\in(0,1]
arbitrarily.
For all
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
,
r\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}]
,

\theta\in(0,\frac{1}{8}]
,
and
(v_{r},q_{r})
satisfying (
Sr
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\Big)\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};r),

(3.20)

where
C
depends only on
L
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

By the regularity of the Stokes equations in flat domains,

\displaystyle v_{r}\in C^{1,\alpha}(Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2})\,,\hskip 20.00003ptq_{r}\in C^{0,\alpha}(Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}).

Let
{\bf e}_{3}=(0,0,1)
. The boundary
C^{1,\alpha}
estimate of
v_{r}
on
\{x_{3}=-\varepsilon\}
implies

\displaystyle|v_{r}(x)-v_{r}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})-(x_{3}+\varepsilon)\partial_{3}v_{r}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|\leq C\frac{|x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3}|^{1+\alpha}}{r^{\alpha}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Note that
\partial_{3}v_{r,3}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})=0
by the condition
\nabla\cdot v_{r}=0
. Thus, from
v_{r}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})=0
, there exists
\widetilde{P}(x)=(\partial_{3}v_{r,1}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3}),\partial_{3}v_{r,2}(-\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3}),0)x_{3}\in\mathscr{P}_{1}
and

\displaystyle|v_{r}(x)-\widetilde{P}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|\leq C\frac{|x_{3}+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3}|^{1+\alpha}}{r^{\alpha}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2},

for all
x\in Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}
.
Since
v_{r}(x)-\widetilde{P}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})
is a weak solution to (
Sr
) with the same pressure
q_{r}
, by the Caccioppoli inequality (see Lemma
A.3
) in
Q_{2\theta r}^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla\widetilde{P}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\bigg)^{1/2}&\leq\frac{C}{\theta r}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2\theta r}^{\varepsilon}}|v_{r}-\widetilde{P}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\bigg)^{1/2}\\
&\leq C\Big(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

Then the observation
\widetilde{P}(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})=\widetilde{P}(x)+\varepsilon(\nabla\widetilde{P}){\bf e}_{3}
yields

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\theta r}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla\widetilde{P}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\Big(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.21)

The
C^{0,\alpha}
estimate of
q_{r}
implies

\displaystyle|q_{r}(x)-q_{r}(0)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|x|^{\alpha}}{r^{\alpha}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}q_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Then by the Bogovskii lemma
in a Lipschitz domain
Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}
,
we have

\displaystyle|q_{r}(x)-q_{r}(0)|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|x|^{\alpha}}{r^{\alpha}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r/2}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2},

which results in

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\theta sr}^{\varepsilon}}q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{\theta tr}^{\varepsilon}}q_{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{\alpha}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{r/2}^{\varepsilon}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.22)

Hence on the one hand, by (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) we see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)&\leq C\Big(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

(3.23)

On the other hand, since
v_{r}(x)-P(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})
, for any
P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}
, is a weak solution to (
Sr
) with the same pressure
q_{r}
and
P(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})=P(x)+\varepsilon(\nabla P){\bf e}_{3}
, we may apply (
3.23
) to

v_{r}(x)-P(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3})
and obtain

\widetilde{H}(v_{r}-P(x+\varepsilon{\bf e}_{3}),q_{r};\theta r)\leq C\Big(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-1}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)\Big)\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.24)

In particular, we may choose
P=P_{*}
that minimizes

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Then, it is clear that

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{r}}|\nabla v_{r}-\nabla P_{*}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq\widetilde{H}(v_{r},q_{r};r).

(3.25)

Then the estimate (
3.20
) follows from (
3.24
) with
P=P_{*}
and (
3.25
). The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
\alpha\in(0,1]
be the number in Lemma
3.5
. For all
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
,
r\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}]
,

\theta\in(0,\frac{1}{8}]
,
and
(v_{r},q_{r})
satisfying (
Sr
),

\displaystyle H(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{\alpha}H(v_{r},q_{r};2r)+C\theta^{-5/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/2}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2},

(3.26)

where
C
depends only on
L
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

The estimate (
3.26
) follows readily from Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
3.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon})
be a weak solution of (
Sε
)
and let
\alpha\in(0,1]
be the number in Lemma
3.5
. For all
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{32}]
,
r\in[2\varepsilon,\frac{1}{16}]
and
\theta\in(0,\frac{1}{8}]
,

\displaystyle H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{\alpha}H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};2r)+C\theta^{-3}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/12}\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};{16r})

(3.27)

\displaystyle+C\theta^{-3}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{10r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3},

where
C
depends only on
L
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

The triangle inequality and Lemma
3.6
imply

\displaystyle H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq H(v_{r},q_{r};\theta r)+H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};\theta r)

(3.28)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{\alpha}H(v_{r},q_{r};2r)+H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle+C\theta^{-5/2}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/2}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{\varepsilon}_{2r}}|\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{\alpha}H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};2r)

\displaystyle+C\theta^{\alpha}H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};r)+H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};\theta r)

\displaystyle+C{\theta^{-5/2}}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/2}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{{4r},+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2},

where in the last line the energy estimate of (
Sr
) is applied. By the definition of
H
, we find

\displaystyle\theta^{\alpha}H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};r)+H(u^{\varepsilon}-v_{r},p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r};\theta r)

(3.29)

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{-3})

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\sup_{\rho\in[1/16,1/4]}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\rho r,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|\bigg\}.

The Poincaré inequality and Lemma
3.2
imply

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\sup_{\rho\in[1/16,1/4]}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\rho r,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|

(3.30)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg\{\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla v_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}(p^{\varepsilon}-q_{r})|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{1/12}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{5r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{4r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}.

Now from (
3.28
) to (
3.30
), we obtain the desired estimate (
3.27
)
by the definition of
\Phi
in (
3.8
). This completes the proof.
∎

3.3.
Iteration

In the following two lemmas, we prove some properties of
H
and
\Phi
needed when iterating (
3.27
).

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon})
be a weak solution of (
Sε
). There exists a function
h(r)
defined on
[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}]
such that

\displaystyle h(r)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)+\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)\big),

(3.31)

\displaystyle\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)+h(r)\big),

(3.32)

\displaystyle\sup_{r_{1},r_{2}\in[r,2r]}|h(r_{1})-h(r_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq CH(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};2r).

(3.33)

Here
C
depends only on
L
. Notice that the function
h
depends on
u^{\varepsilon}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
51
, Lemma 6.1]
and hence we provide the outline of the proof. Let
P_{r}\in\mathscr{P}_{1}
be such that

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla P_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}=\inf_{P\in\mathscr{P}_{1}}\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla P|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

We define

\displaystyle h(r)=|\nabla P_{r}|,\hskip 10.00002ptr\in\big[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}\big].

Then the inequality (
3.31
) follows from

\displaystyle h(r)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla P_{r}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)+\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)\big)

and (
3.32
) is trivial by definition. For (
3.33
), we observe that for any
r_{1},r_{2}\in[r,2r]

\displaystyle|h(r_{1})-h(r_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla P_{r_{1}}-\nabla P_{r_{2}}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq CH(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};2r).

This completes the proof of Lemma
3.8
.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
L\in(0,\infty)
and
\Omega
be a bumpy John domain with constant
L
according to Definition
1.2
. Let
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon})
be a weak solution of (
Sε
). Then for
\varepsilon\in(0,{\frac{1}{8}}]
,
r\in[{2\varepsilon},\frac{1}{4}]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[r,2r]}\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\tau)\leq C\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};2r)+\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{2r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3},

(3.34)

where
C
depends only on
L
.

Proof.

Let
\tau\in[r,2r]
. A simple computation implies

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\tau,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq C\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{2r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(3.35)

For the pressure estimate, by a similar argument as in (
3.6
),

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[1/16,1/4]}\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{s\tau,+}}p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{t\tau,+}}p^{\varepsilon}\bigg|

(3.36)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r/2,+}}p^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg\{\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|F^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\},

where we need to assume
r\geq 2\varepsilon
.
Then the assertion (
3.34
) follows from (
3.35
), (
3.36
) and Lemma
2.1
(iii).
∎

We now state the iteration lemma. Its
proof is given in Appendix
C
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
H,\Phi,h:(0,1]\to[0,\infty)
be nonnegative functions.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{48}]
. Suppose that there exist positive constants
C_{0}
,
B_{0}
,
\alpha,\beta
and
\theta\in(0,{\frac{1}{8}}]
so that

\displaystyle H(\theta r)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}H({2r})+C_{0}\Big(\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{r}\big)^{\alpha}\Phi(16r)+B_{0}r^{\beta}\Big),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{16}],

(3.37a)

\displaystyle H(r)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Phi(r),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}],

(3.37b)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[r,2r]}\Phi(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(\Phi(2r)+B_{0}r^{\beta}\big),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}],

(3.37c)

\displaystyle h(r)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(H(r)+\Phi(r)\big),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}],

(3.37d)

\displaystyle\Phi(r)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(H(r)+h(r)\big),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}],

(3.37e)

\displaystyle\sup_{r_{1},r_{2}\in[r,2r]}|h(r_{1})-h(r_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}H(2r),\hskip 10.00002ptr\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}].

(3.37f)

Then,

\int_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\frac{H(t)}{t}\,{\rm d}t+\sup_{r\in[\varepsilon,1/2]}\Phi(r)\leq C(\Phi(\frac{1}{2})+B_{0}),

(3.38)

where the constant
C
depends only on
C_{0},\alpha,\beta
and
\theta
.

Proof of Theorem
A
:

In the following proof, we actually only need to show (
1.2
) for the case
N_{0}\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/N_{1}
for some
N_{0},N_{1}\geq 2
. The case
1/2\geq r\geq 1/N_{1}
follows trivially by enlarging the size of the cube and a standard pressure estimate (see Remark
3.3
); the case
\varepsilon\leq r\leq N_{0}\varepsilon
follows from the case
r=N_{0}\varepsilon
. From the previous lemmas, we can choose
N_{0}=4
and
N_{1}=16
. Hence, we may assume without loss of generality that
r\in[4\varepsilon,\frac{1}{16}]
.

We apply Lemma
3.10
to
H(r)=H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)
and
\Phi(r)=\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)
.
Choose
\theta
sufficiently small so that we have
C\theta^{\alpha}\leq 1/2
in (
3.27
) in Lemma
3.7
.
We need to verify the conditions in Lemma
3.10
. Note that (
3.37b
) is obvious and (
3.37d
)-(
3.37f
) follow from Lemma
3.8
. To verify (
3.37a
) from Lemma
3.7
and verify (
3.37c
) from Lemma
3.9
(with
\varepsilon
replaced by
4\varepsilon
), it suffices to note that Theorem
2.8
implies

\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{{Q_{r}}}|\mathcal{M}^{2}_{\varepsilon}[F^{\varepsilon}]|^{3}\bigg)^{1/3}\leq C(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta})r^{\beta},

(3.39)

for any
\beta\in(0,2)
,
\delta\in(0,1)
with
\beta+\delta<2
and
r\in[\varepsilon.\frac{1}{2}]
. Hence, we may apply Lemma
3.10
with
B_{0}=C(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta})
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{4\varepsilon}^{1/2}\frac{H(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};t)}{t}dt+\sup_{{{r\in[4\varepsilon,1/2]}}}\Phi(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};r)

(3.40)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\Phi\big(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\frac{1}{2})+(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta}),

where in the last inequality, we have used a standard pressure estimate (see Remark
3.3
) to bound
\Phi\big(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\frac{1}{2})
by
C(M+M^{2})
. Hence, for
r\in[4\varepsilon,\frac{1}{16}]
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\Phi\big(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon};\frac{1}{2})+(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(M+M^{4+2\beta+2\delta}),

which proves the desired estimate of the velocity
u^{\varepsilon}
.

Next, we give an estimate for the pressure. For
r\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}]
, we observe that

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{1/2,+}}p^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}p^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{1/2,+}}p^{\varepsilon}\bigg|.

Using the technique as in (
3.6
) and by the Bogovskii lemma, the desired estimate of
\nabla u^{\varepsilon}
just proved and (
3.39
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}|p^{\varepsilon}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B^{\varepsilon}_{r,+}}p^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}
